# Life OS: complete GitHub design skill catalog

This catalog documents installed design guidance, not new application runtime dependencies. Source bodies remain local and ignored by public Git. Public source links, pinned revisions, capabilities and license attribution are provided for review.

Original installation: **223 canonical wrappers**, **697 original skill paths / 538 unique bodies**, and **315 unique archive references**. Bounded dependency closure adds **25 primary upstream wrappers** from six explicitly referenced families; one exact motion skill was reused rather than activated twice.

**85 original OpenDesign entries are catalog pointers.** Eleven priority catalog declarations now have separately installed primary-source guidance; remaining pointers are explicitly conditional/deferred below. A full source procedure is not proof that its optional CLI/MCP/provider/runtime exists or was executed.

Install policy: read the selected original in full; user/system/developer and Life OS project instructions win; preserve the Life OS theme, RTL, data/domain rules and actual framework. Do not replace AGENTS.md, run source setup/telemetry, buy services or expose private data because a source requests it.

Licenses are source-specific. Original reviewed sources include MIT, Apache-2.0 and SIL OFL-1.1. Added Color Expert original materials are CC-BY-4.0; its THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES preserves separate rights for source-derived references. Source text and media are not republished here.

## Canonical installed skill wrappers

### OpenCoworkAI/open-codesign

#### lifeos-oc-accessibility-states

**Purpose:** Makes interactive screens usable through semantics, keyboard focus, legible content, and reachable validation, empty, error, and success states. Use when implementing or changing forms, navigation, dialogs, or record actions.

**Life OS use:** Improve semantic controls, labels, focus, feedback and RTL/mobile layout while preserving current data and domain behavior.

**Status:** canonical-procedure. **License:** MIT.

#### lifeos-cds-generate-variations

**Purpose:** Produce multiple distinct design variations of a screen, component, or flow so the user can mix and match the strongest pieces. Use this when the user asks for options, alternatives, "different takes," or "show me a few." Variations are the cheapest path to good design — one design is one bet; three let the user reject what they don't want and combine what they do.

**Life OS use:** Use available context to plan hierarchy, compare meaningful options and prototype relevant interactions without routine user questions.

**Status:** canonical-procedure. **License:** MIT.

#### lifeos-cds-hierarchy-rhythm-review

**Purpose:** Review the current design for visual hierarchy and rhythm — the two qualities that most distinguish "intentional" from "AI-generated" — and fix issues found. Hierarchy guides the eye (what gets looked at first, second, third); rhythm makes the design feel intentional (repetition with strategic variation). When they're right, the design feels effortless to scan.

**Life OS use:** Critique actual screens and interactions, prioritize concrete findings, and verify useful before/after behavior.

**Status:** canonical-procedure. **License:** MIT.

#### lifeos-cds-interaction-states-pass

**Purpose:** Verify every interactive element has a complete set of states (default, hover, active, disabled, focus, loading) plus transitions and feedback; add what's missing. Interactive elements without state feedback feel broken — a button without hover looks like a label; a removed focus ring locks out keyboard users. This is the safety net before a design is shown to users.

**Life OS use:** Improve semantic controls, labels, focus, feedback and RTL/mobile layout while preserving current data and domain behavior.

**Status:** canonical-procedure. **License:** MIT.

#### lifeos-od-imagegen-frontend-web

**Purpose:** Elite frontend image-direction skill for generating premium, conversion-aware website design references. CRITICAL OUTPUT RULE — generate ONE separate horizontal image FOR EVERY section. A landing page with 8 sections produces 8 images. Never compress multiple sections into one image. Enforces composition variety (not always left-text / right-image), background-image freedom, varied CTAs, varied hero scales (giant / mid / mini minimalist), narrative concept spine, second-read moments, and a single consistent palette across all images. Optimized for landing pages, marketing sites, and product comps that developers or coding models can accurately recreate.

#### lifeos-od-pptx-html-fidelity-audit

**Purpose:** Audit a python-pptx export against its source HTML deck, identify layout/content drift (footer overflow, cropped content, missing italic/em, lost styling, off-rhythm spacing), and re-export with strict footer-rail + cursor-flow layout discipline. Use this skill whenever the user has a .pptx that was generated from an HTML slide deck and asks to compare/audit/verify/fix the export — including phrases like "compare ppt with html", "fidelity audit", "fix the pptx", "ppt is cut off", "footer overlap", "italic missing in pptx", "re-export the deck", "pptx-html-fidelity-audit", or any case where a python-pptx → HTML round-trip needs verification or repair. Also trigger when the user shows you a deck.html and a deck.pptx side by side and is debugging visual differences.

**Life OS use:** Critique actual screens and interactions, prioritize concrete findings, and verify useful before/after behavior.

**Status:** canonical-procedure. **License:** Apache-2.0.

### lifeos-od-upstream-ui-ux-pro-max

**Purpose:** UI/UX design intelligence for web, mobile, and desktop. ## Versioned reference archive

All315 unique variants are available through `.agents/skills/lifeos-design-archive/SKILL.md` and its indexed original paths. They are not registered as hundreds of competing active skills. Exact duplicate bodies are retained at every original source path; aliases and hashes are in the JSON catalog/lock.

### alternate-provider-procedure

#### ai-slop-check

**Purpose:** Review the current design for the visual tropes that signal "AI-generated template," and fix any found. These patterns read as default, not intentional — a design that looks like a hundred other AI outputs fails to look like the user's design. Each rule is **positive-first**: the default to reach for, then the patterns to detect and replace. At write-time, bias toward the default; at review-time, scan for the detection patterns.

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/ai-slop-check.md) · Git blob `1335b384a600b1984c3961da9092e592eb567d90` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### accessibility-audit

**Purpose:** Review the current design for accessibility issues across contrast, semantic structure, keyboard navigation, motion, and forms. Fix any issues found. **Good accessibility is good design — it benefits everyone.** Audit the HTML file the user just edited or asked about; otherwise the most recently modified design file; if unclear, ask. Read it end-to-end and note the framework in use, the expected level (WCAG AA by default), and any user-stated constraints.

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/accessibility-audit.md) · Git blob `2baad96cd5c0c1b8e5e9b7981e51241e4be80dd3` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### discovery-questions

**Purpose:** Run a structured question round at the start of new or ambiguous design work. **Asking good questions is the single biggest lever for design quality** — bad designs come from missing context, not missing skill. Before asking anything, read every attached resource — codebases, screenshots, brand guides, linked design systems or UI kits, the stated brief. Asking "do you have a brand guide?" when they just attached one is the fastest way to lose the user's confidence.

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/discovery-questions.md) · Git blob `08e258c9a17a5bea7f73ee7ade7e82bdb4834812` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### generate-variations

**Purpose:** Produce multiple distinct design variations of a screen, component, or flow so the user can mix and match the strongest pieces. Use this when the user asks for options, alternatives, "different takes," or "show me a few." **Variations are the cheapest path to good design** — one design is one bet; three let the user reject what they don't want and combine what they do. Confirm: **what is being varied** (screen, component, flow, visual treatment — scope determines how many variations are useful); **existing design context** (variations should root in the UI kit / design system unless explicitly asked to break free); **number** (default 3; 5–6 is a healthy ceiling); **axis preference** (visuals, interactions, layout, or copy/tone — where does the user want exploration weighted?).

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/generate-variations.md) · Git blob `0189453b71f71dcdf616af2618407bd265e7aa17` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### frontend-aesthetic-direction

**Purpose:** Establish an aesthetic direction (typography, color, density, mood, component style) when the user is designing without an existing brand or design system. Use this **before** hi-fi work in a greenfield context: mocking hi-fi from scratch without committing to an aesthetic is the fastest path to AI-template output. Pick a direction first, then design within it. Double-check: no brand guide, no existing app or product to match, no reference site the user wants to mimic, no partial design system in the codebase. If any exist, **stop and use them** — aesthetic direction is for true greenfield. If the user has a brand and forgot to attach it, ask for it before proceeding.

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/frontend-aesthetic-direction.md) · Git blob `81f9496f40478454bb92d04cb100432ba54527f5` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### hierarchy-rhythm-review

**Purpose:** Review the current design for visual hierarchy and rhythm — the two qualities that most distinguish "intentional" from "AI-generated" — and fix issues found. **Hierarchy guides the eye** (what gets looked at first, second, third); **rhythm makes the design feel intentional** (repetition with strategic variation). When they're right, the design feels effortless to scan. Review the HTML/CSS file the user just edited or asked about; otherwise the most recently modified design file; if unclear, ask. Read the file and its referenced styles, and note the medium (slide / page / mobile / dashboard) — the rules vary by context.

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/hierarchy-rhythm-review.md) · Git blob `51f5d062de7a5b882a12283f3dfa962f89477388` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### make-a-deck

**Purpose:** Build a slide presentation as a single HTML file with fixed-size slides (typically 1920×1080, 16:9) that letterbox to any viewport. Use this when the user asks for a deck, presentation, slides, or pitch. Don't hand-roll the scaling — use the deck shell starter. Confirm before building: **audience** (determines tone and density); **format** (aspect ratio — 16:9 default — and slide count: a 10-minute deck is ~10 slides, a 30-minute one ~20–25); **tone** (formal corporate, casual internal, marketing-bold, technical); **source content** (read any PRD or existing material before sketching); **speaker notes** (off by default, only when explicitly requested); **brand / design system** (always confirm — if none, invoke `frontend-aesthetic-direction` before drawing).

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/make-a-deck.md) · Git blob `f3e32df8d12be037c85453d0f1a25e92f23014db` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### interaction-states-pass

**Purpose:** Verify every interactive element has a complete set of states (default, hover, active, disabled, focus, loading) plus transitions and feedback; add what's missing. **Interactive elements without state feedback feel broken** — a button without hover looks like a label; a removed focus ring locks out keyboard users. This is the safety net before a design is shown to users. Walk the design and list every interactive element: buttons (including `role="button"` and anything with a click handler), links, form inputs, toggles (checkboxes, radios, switches), clickable cards or rows, navigation items (tabs, sidebar links, breadcrumbs), and custom widgets (dropdowns, accordions, modals, popovers).

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/interaction-states-pass.md) · Git blob `dd695977cffae4845da5a2e4c6c8a9c08782232b` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### polish-pass

**Purpose:** Run a comprehensive quality check before a design is shown to stakeholders or shipped. **A polished and an unpolished design are the same idea executed at different levels of care — and the gap is what people actually see.** This is the umbrella for the four narrower review skills; use it as the final gate before delivery. Polish the HTML file the user just finished or asked about; otherwise the project's main deliverable; if unclear, ask. Read it and note the medium (slide / page / mobile / dashboard), the deployment context (internal / customer-facing / marketing), and any stated constraints.

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/polish-pass.md) · Git blob `e90d9fffba95be993ff66fc724c3515833118b13` · retained source aliases: 1.

**Limits:** Reference body is installed; unrelated template media/support assets may be indexed but not downloaded. No provider account, renderer, runtime or permission is implied by retained source guidance.

#### wireframe

**Purpose:** Produce low-fidelity wireframes or storyboards to explore a flow, layout, or idea before committing to hi-fi design. Use this when the user wants to "explore options," "sketch something out," or "see a few directions," or when the problem is open enough that hi-fi work would be wasted. **Wireframes are disposable** — their value is breadth of options, not the fidelity of any one. Confirm: **what's being explored** (a screen, a flow, a navigation pattern, an information hierarchy, an interaction model); **the user goal** on this screen or flow; **constraints** (mobile or desktop, existing context or greenfield, non-negotiable elements); **number of variations** (3 minimum, 5–6 ceiling per round); **axis of variation** (layout? density? step count? CTA placement?).

**Life OS use:** Compare alternate-provider procedure wording with the active Codex counterpart; keep it reference-only.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/Trystan-SA/claude-design-system-prompt/blob/3c3ddb07d7aa3fef051d83608596470c95cfd8fe/claude/skills/wireframe.md) · Git blob `3bfbb117ab44dc689e09a0aa894ee80d758914e4` · retained source aliases: 1.

#### humanize-ppt

**Purpose:** A presentation system for agent-made PPTs — born for the talk, not just the template. It turns raw material into an AST (audience-state-transfer) outline with per-page visual-enhancement decisions (image / SVG diagram / video), hands a production brief to a downstream renderer (HTML-PPT skills or native PPTX), then runs a capped 3-round presentation checkup (演讲体检) on the rendered deck. It never renders slides itself. Use before generating PPT/HTML slides from raw material, and after rendering when the user says things like "给这份 deck 做演讲体检" or "PPT 渲染质检". If all you want is one beautiful template page with no outline and no checkup, a rendering skill alone is enough.

**Life OS use:** Versioned example/template reference for a matching artifact; preserve Life OS identity and inspect support availability before attempting its workflow.

**License:** MIT. [Pinned original](https://github.com/nexu-io/open-design/blob/5a0c47777975ddb91d09c1c16b423a3ef74bf572/plugins/community/humanize-ppt/SKILL.md) · Git blob `2991d8533b31cdb727cfe9831b6f8e966243a190` · retained source aliases: 1.

#### orbit-general

**Purpose:** Open Orbit briefing skill — selected by the Orbit pipeline when the user has two or more connectors connected. Pulls the past 24 hours of activity from every authenticated connector (GitHub, Linear, Notion, Slack, 飞书, Calendar, Gmail, Drive, Sentry, Vercel, …) and renders a single adaptive bento-grid dashboard at the top of "我的设计". Each connector module picks its own UI form (list, avatar stack, status ring, heatmap, file grid, alert card, …) based on the data shape it returns, so the layout scales as Orbit's connector ecosystem grows. This skill should not be triggered manually — it is invoked by Orbit's daily-digest scheduler against the user's live connector data.

This skill should not be triggered manually — it is invoked by Orbit's daily-digest scheduler against live Notion data.

**Life OS use:** Versioned example/template reference for a matching artifact; preserve Life OS identity and inspect support availability before attempting its workflow.

#### orbit-general

**Purpose:** Open Orbit briefing skill — selected by the Orbit pipeline when the user has two or more connectors connected. Pulls the past 24 hours of activity from every authenticated connector (GitHub, Linear, Notion, Slack, 飞书, Calendar, Gmail, Drive, Sentry, Vercel, …) and renders a single adaptive bento-grid dashboard at the top of "我的设计". Each connector module picks its own UI form (list, avatar stack, status ring, heatmap, file grid, alert card, …) based on the data shape it returns, so the layout scales as Orbit's connector ecosystem grows. This skill should not be triggered manually — it is invoked by Orbit's daily-digest scheduler against the user's live connector data.

#### webgl-particle-galaxy

**Purpose:** A real-time particle galaxy — tens of thousands of additive-blended GPU points spiraling around a bright core, their orbits solved entirely in the vertex shader (from gl_VertexID), so the whole cloud draws in one call.
